Given a length n, b length 2n-1 (convolution). Move x from p to q (p source, q dest), with 0<=x<=a[p]. New a: a[p]-=x, a[q]+=x. How update b efficiently?
Original contributions of p and q:
For all k:
- p-p: lag 2p: a[p]^2 changes to (a_p-x)^2 => Δ=-2a_p x + x^2.
- q-q: lag 2q: a_q^2 changes to (a_q+x)^2 => Δ=2a_q x + x^2.
- p-q and q-p if p!=q: lag p+q: original 2 a_p a_q, new 2(a_p-x)(a_q+x)=original +2x(a_q-a_p)-2x^2. If p==q no (same as p-p).
- Other pairs involving p with r not p,q: for lag p+r and r+p (same coefficient? Need p,q distinct). Original terms a_p a_r and a_r a_p each appear if both indices valid. When a_p decreases by x, each term changes -x a_r, total for lag p+r: -x a_r? Wait linear convolution coefficient b_k = Σ_{i=0}^n-1 a_i a_{k-i}. For a_p change δ_p=-x: Δ=-x Σ_{r: p+r=k? Actually b_k includes term a_p a_{k-p} (if 0≤k-p<n) and when r=p? It also includes a_{k-p} a_p? The sum is over all i, so term i=p gives a_p a_{k-p}; term i=k-p gives a_{k-p} a_p if different, so two terms unless i=k-p (p + (k-p)=k always, if k-p=p => k=2p). Let's use derivative method: b_k = Σ_i a_i a_{k-i}. Treat a_i independent; partial derivative wrt a_p is a_{k-p} if 0≤k-p<n. For single variable changes, first-order Δ ≈ Σ δ_i a_{k-i}. But exact for two changes includes cross term δ_p δ_q if p+q=k and δ_p +? Let's derive exact:
b_k' = Σ_i (a_i + δ_i) (a_{k-i}+δ_{k-i}) where δ_i nonzero p,q.
= b_k + Σ_i δ_i a_{k-i} + Σ_i a_i δ_{k-i} + Σ_i δ_i δ_{k-i}.
First two sums equal 2 Σ_i δ_i a_{k-i} except when? Since indices p,q: For k-p valid: δ_p a_{k-p}; for k-q valid: δ_q a_{k-q}. Second sum is for i such that k-i=p => i=k-p: a_i δ_p if k-p valid; plus i=k-q: a_{k-q} δ_q. Thus combined linear = 2(δ_p a_{k-p}+δ_q a_{k-q}) if valid? Check double counting when k-p=q? Suppose k-p = q. Then first sum term δ_p a_q; second sum term a_p δ_q? They are distinct variables. The coefficient b has pair p,q counted twice. New difference from p,q pair: (a_p+δ_p)(a_q+δ_q) etc. Linear terms indeed two. Quadratic term: Σ_i δ_i δ_{k-i}. Nonzero if {i,k-i}={p,q}: either i=p,k-i=q => k=p+q and δ_p δ_q; or i=q,k-i=p also same? The sum over i includes both i=p and i=q if p≠q and k=p+q, giving 2δ_pδ_q. But direct pair product difference had 2x( a_q-a_p)-2x^2, linear 2x(a_q-a_p) (δ_p=-x,δ_q=x) and quadratic -2x^2. δ_pδ_q=-x^2, times 2 = -2x^2 good. If k=2p, i=p,k-i=p: quadratic δ_p^2; direct p-p change -2a_p x+x^2: linear 2δ_p a_p=-2a_p x, quadratic δ_p^2=x^2. Good. If k=2q quadratic δ_q^2=x^2. So exact formula:
δ_p=-x, δ_q=x.
For each k, Δb_k =
 linear: 2*( -x * a[k-p] if 0<=k-p<n + x * a[k-q] if 0<=k-q<n )
 quadratic: if k==2p: +x^2; if k==2q: +x^2; if p!=q and k==p+q: +2*(-x)*(x) = -2x^2.
But if p==q, no, no move.
So we can update b by constructing vector delta_len 2n-1. Naively for each move, fill delta for k in union of ranges [p, p+n-1] for p and [q, q+n-1] for q (each length n) -> O(n). Then test. But we can do vectorized:
delta = np.zeros(2n-1); delta[p:p+n] += -2*x*a[0:n-p]? Need reverse.
Specifically linear_p for k from p to p+n-1: -2x * a[k-p]? Wait linear = 2(δ_p a_{k-p}+δ_q a_{k-q}) = -2x a_{k-p} + 2x a_{k-q}. For k in [p, p+n-1], add -2x*a[k-p]. For k in [q, q+n-1], add +2x*a[k-q]. Then quadratics at points. This is O(n) vector slices with assignments.
But if we try many x per pair, we can compute linear delta L_k and quadratic Q_k (0, +1, -2, +1). Then for candidate x, new max = max(b + x L + x^2 Q). Q has few nonzero. We can precompute L vector O(n). To find best x, we can sample many x and use vectorized max? If S samples, (S x (2n)) memory maybe okay for S=32,n=4096 ~12M floats 96MB too high. Instead evaluate sequentially in Python loop for S=16 using numpy max O(n) each => 16*8191=131k ops, okay for few thousand moves. Could be slow if too many. Could optimize by checking only top peak candidates? Since max of b + affine functions often determined by current peaks or those affected. But vectorized max of length 8192 is fast (C). 16*8k=130k, in C ~ microseconds? NumPy overhead ~10-20us, 16 ~200us, plus L fill. Could support ~50k moves/s? Maybe less due Python. 2400s can do millions? Need careful.

Could use "steepest descent pair" based on subgradient: We want move from i to j that decreases max. The subgradient at active peaks: vector g_i = Σ_{active k} w_k a_{k-i}. Transfer derivative for small x from i to j: δb_k = x(a_{k-j} - a_{k-i}); change to objective approx x(g_j - g_i). To decrease, move from high g_i to low g_j (if i source). This is similar.    - g zeros; for each k,w: add w * a[segment] to corresponding positions. Need mapping: k lag: indices i in [max(0,k-n+1), min(n-1,k)]. `a[0:k-js+1]` if reverse? b_k = Σ_{i=js}^{je} a[i] * a[k-i]. Direct pair line search details:
```
def eval_move(a,b,p,q,x,max_candidates?)
    L=2*n-1
    diff = np.zeros(L)
    diff[p:p+n] -= a[0:n-p]
    diff[q:q+n] += a[0:n-q]
    # For each fraction f:
        x=f*a[p]
        np.multiply(diff, 2.0*x, out=lin)
        np.add(b, lin, out=tmp)
        val=tmp.max()
        # adjust quadratic
        if 0 <= 2*p < L: val = max(val, tmp[2*p]+x*x)  # tmp[2p] didn't include qfac
        if 0 <= 2*q < L: val = max(val, tmp[2*q]+x*x)
        if p!=q:
             m=p+q
             val = max(val, tmp[m]-2*x*x)
        obj=2*n*val
```
But note `tmp` after `np.add` modified; for next candidate we reuse, okay. But when adjusting val, `tmp[m]` is base without qfac; if qfac point also in diff linear, base includes linear. Good.

```
def pair_line_search(a,b,p,q,cur_obj,fracs=None):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    mass=a[p]
    if mass <= 1e-15: return None
    diff=np.zeros(L)
    diff[p:p+n] -= a[0:n-p]
    diff[q:q+n] += a[0:n-q]
    best_obj=cur_obj; best_x=0.0; best_b=None
    if fracs is None: fracs=...
    lin=np.empty(L); tmp=np.empty(L)
    for frac in fracs:
       x=frac*mass
       if x<=0: continue
       np.multiply(diff, 2.0*x, out=lin)
       np.add(b, lin, out=tmp)
       val=tmp.max()
       val = max(val, tmp[2*p] + x*x)
       val = max(val, tmp[2*q] + x*x)
       if p != q:
           m=p+q
           val = max(val, tmp[m] - 2.0*x*x)
       obj=2.0*n*val
       if obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
           best_obj=obj; best_x=x; # store copy of tmp? We'll recompute later
    if best_x==0: return None
    # recompute best_b to return
    lin... tmp...
    return best_obj,best_x,tmp.copy()
```
But if best fraction was not last, tmp currently for last. Recompute with best_x.

```

Function `g_from_active(a,b,active_count=12, thresh=0.99)`:
```
def active_gradient(a,b, max_active=12, thresh=0.99):
    n=len(a); maxB=b.max()
    if maxB<=0: return np.zeros(n)
    idx=np.where(b >= max(thresh*maxB, maxB*0.9?))
    if len(idx) > max_active:
       # top max_active by b
       idx = np.sort(idx[np.argpartition(b[idx], -max_active)[-max_active:]])
    w = np.ones(len(idx))/len(idx)
    g=np.zeros(n)
    for ii,kk in enumerate(idx):
       k=int(kk); wt=float(w[ii])
       js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
       g[js:je+1] += wt * a[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
    return g
```
 Could include temperature: weights = (b[idx]/maxB)^p? If near peaks, similar. uniform.

def top_indices_neg(g, count, valid_mask):
    # For valid indices, set invalid g=-inf, argpartition
    gv=g.copy(); gv[~valid_mask]=-np.inf
    return np.argpartition(-gv, min(count,n-1))[:count]
```

Function `greedy_pair_optimize(a, time_limit, rng, max_pairs=4, random_prob=0.05, verbose=False)`:
```
def greedy_pair_optimize(a, time_limit, rng, ...):
    n=len(a); a=normalize; b=fft_convolve(a)
    cur_obj=2*n*b.max(); best_a=a.copy(); best_b=b.copy(); best_obj=cur_obj
    t0=time.monotonic(); no_improve=0; iter=0
    fracs = (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.55,0.8,0.97)
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       active=g_from_active(a,b, max_active=10, thresh=0.99)
       # generate candidate pairs
       pairs=[]
       if rng.random() < random_prob:
          valid_idx=...
          src=random; dst=random; pairs.append
       else:
          srcs=top...; dsts=top...; add up to 6 unique
       best_pair=None
       for (p,q) in pairs:
          res=pair_line_search(a,b,p,q,cur_obj,fracs)
          if res and (best_pair is None or res[0] < best_pair[0]): best_pair=res
       if best_pair and best_pair[0] < cur_obj - 1e-12:
          obj,x,new_b=best_pair
          a[p]-=x; a[q]+=x; if a[p]<0:a[p]=0; a[q]+=... (ensure sum)
          b=new_b
          cur_obj=obj
          no_improve=0
          if cur_obj < best_obj:
             best_obj=cur_obj; best_a=a.copy(); best_b=b.copy(); maybe print if verbose
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve > 50:
              # perturb: random small move (may worsen) to escape
              # choose random source with mass, random dest, small fraction 0.05, apply if finite
              # or reset a to best? If reset, no_improve=0.
              # Better random walk: pick pair random, line search allows only improvements; if none worse not accepted. For escape, accept random small move with no objective? We can apply a tiny random perturbation: transfer eps mass.
              eps = max(1e-6, 0.01*(a.mean()))
              idxs=np.nonzero(a>2*eps)[0]; if len>1:
                 p=rng.choice(idxs); q=rng.integers(n); if p!=q and a[p]>eps:
                    x=eps; a[p]-=x; a[q]+=x; b=fft_convolve(a); cur_obj=...
                    no_improve=0
              if no_improve>100:
                  a=best_a.copy(); b=best_b.copy(); cur_obj=best_obj; no_improve=0
       iter+=1
       if iter % 2000: recompute b=fft_convolve(a); cur_obj=...
    # final maybe recompute
    if safe? return best_a, best_obj
```
But random perturbation may drastically worsen; but small. If stuck many, reset to best.

Function `lp_optimize(a, time_limit, max_calls=15, t_grid=...)`:
```
def lp_optimize(a,time_limit):
   n=len(a); if n>1024 return a
   a=normalize; b=fft; cur_obj; best
   calls=0
   while time left and calls<max_calls:
      M=b.max()*(1+1e-9)+1e-15
      g=lp_direction(a,M)
      if g is None: break
      g=normalize(g)
      # line search t
      best_t=0; best_obj=cur_obj
      for t in (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.7,0.9):
          h=(1-t)*a + t*g
          hh=h/sum
          bb=fft_convolve(hh); obj=2n*bb.max()
          if obj<best_obj: ...
      if best_t>0:
          a=(1-best_t)*a+best_t*g; normalize; b=fft; cur_obj=best_obj; if < best update
      else: maybe once break? Try different g? If no improve, break or change M margin.
      calls+=1
   return best_a
```

Implement `get_parent_sequence()`:
```
def get_parent_sequence():
   try:
      p=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
      p=np.clip(p,0,1000)
      if p.size>0 and np.isfinite(p).all():
         s=p.sum()
         if s>1e-10: return p/s
   except Exception: pass
   return None
```

Interpolation to n:
```
def interpolate_sequence(parent,n):
   x_old=np.linspace(0,1,parent.size)
   x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   out=np.interp(x_new,x_old,parent)
   out=np.maximum(out,0)
   if out.sum()>0: out/=out.sum()
   else: out=np.ones(n)/n
   return out
```
But parent endpoints maybe nonzero; interpolation okay.

def generate_starts(n, parent, rng):
    starts=[]
    if parent is not None:
       starts.append(('parent', interpolate_sequence(parent,n)))
    starts.append(('uniform', np.ones(n)/n))
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    t=(x+0.5)/n
    # sine broad: (1+cos(pi*(x-(n-1)/2)/(n-1)))? Maybe use sin^p with zeros endpoints
    xs=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    for p in [0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0]:
       base=np.sin(np.pi*(np.arange(n)+1)/(n+1))
       base=np.maximum(base,1e-12)**p
       starts.append((f'sin{p}', base/base.sum()))
    # one-sided geometrics with ratios near 1: r = exp(-alpha/n)
    for alpha in [0.1,0.3,0.7,1.5,3.0]:
       r=math.exp(-alpha/n)
       w=r**x  # may underflow for large n? r**x for x up to 2048, alpha=3 => exp(-3/n *2048)=exp(-3) ok; alpha maybe 20? no.
       w=np.maximum(w,0); normalize
    # symmetric sum of geometrics? maybe
    # Gaussian centered
    for sigma in [0.15,0.25,0.4]:
       g=np.exp(-0.5*((x-(n-1)/2)/(sigma*(n-1)))**2)
    # random
    for alpha in [0.1,1.0,10.0]: rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*alpha)? Dirichlet for n=2048 maybe alpha small okay, but cost O(n).
    return starts
```

def safe_evaluate(a):
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
       arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
       s=arr.sum()
       if s<0.01: return float('inf')
       val=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
       return float(val)
    except Exception:
       # fallback direct
       arr=np.maximum(np.asarray(a,float),0)
       s=arr.sum()
       if s<1e-12: return inf
       b=fft_convolve(arr/s)
       return 2*len(arr)*b.max()
```
But fallback if evaluate_sequence not defined might NameError inside try? It will be caught? NameError caught by Exception? Yes. But if not imported, fallback. OK.

Now LP line search. Building C each call. Need ensure `linprog` returns optimal. For large n, `sum_g` might be `inf` due unbounded? Check feasible/bounded. If status not 0, return None.

```
def lp_direction(f, M):
    n=f.size
    if n > 1200: return None
    from scipy.optimize import linprog  # top-level try? Can't import inside? top-level allowed.
    C=np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
    for k in range(2*n-1):
       js = max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
       if js <= je:
          C[k, js:je+1] = f[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
    c = -np.ones(n)
    res = linprog(c, A_ub=C, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M), bounds=(0, None), method='highs', options={'output_flag':0, 'presolve': True})
    if res is None or res.status not in (0,2): return None
    g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
    g = np.maximum(g,0)
    s=g.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return None
    return g/s
```
Status 2 is unbounded? Actually HiGHS status 2 = optimal infeasible? Let's recall scipy linprog status: 0 optimal, 1 failure, 2 infeasible, 3 unbounded, 4 numerical. So only 0. Use `if res.status != 0: return None`. For status 2 infeasible due roundoff; maybe retry with larger M? In lp_opt loop factor.
Options: `options={'output_flag': 0, 'presolve': True}`. Could set `maxiter=50000`. But HiGHS may not accept unknown? `maxiter` accepted. Use `options={'output_flag': 0, 'presolve': True, 'maxiter': 200000}`.

Now implement `pair_anneal` properly.

```
def pair_anneal(a, time_limit, rng, max_pairs=3):
    n=len(a); a=normalize; b=fft; cur_obj=2n*b.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_b=b.copy(); best_obj=cur_obj
    t0=time.monotonic(); fracs = (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.7)
    # maybe recompute g every 20
    iter=0
    while time left:
       if iter % 20 ==0:
          g=compute_direction(a,b, rc=8)
       # choose pair
       if rng.random() < 0.5:
          if g is None: source random; dest random
          else:
             # source from top 20% or weighted by g
             valid = np.flatnonzero(a > max(1e-9,1e-4/n))
             if len(valid)==0: valid=np.arange(n)
             # choose source index from valid with probability proportional to np.maximum(g[valid],0)
             wsrc = np.maximum(g[valid], 0.0) + 1e-12
             wsrc /= wsrc.sum()
             p = int(valid[rng.choice(len(valid), p=wsrc)])
             # dest: low g or random
             if rng.random()<0.5:
                q = int(valid[rng.choice(len(valid))])  # maybe low? Use uniform
             else:
                # bottom of g all indices
                q = int(np.argpartition(g, 10)[0]) maybe
       else:
          p=int(rng.integers(n)); q=int(rng.integers(n))
       if p==q: continue
       res=pair_line_search(a,b,p,q,cur_obj,fracs,positive_only=True)
       if res is None: continue
       cand_obj,x,new_b=res
       delta=cand_obj-cur_obj
       frac_time=min(1.0,(time.monotonic()-t0)/max(time_limit,1.0))
       T=1e-4*(1-frac_time)**2 + 1e-6
       if delta < 0 or rng.random() < math.exp(-delta / T):
           # apply
           a[p]=max(0,a[p]-x); a[q]+=x
           b=new_b.copy()
           cur_obj=cand_obj
           if cur_obj < best_obj:
              best_obj=cur_obj; best_a=a.copy()
       iter+=1
       if iter % 2000 ==0: recompute
    if time safe: maybe safe? return best_a,best_obj
```
But `rng.choice(len(valid), p=wsrc)` requires p sum 1. If wsrc sum 0, uniform. For large n, generating wsrc each 20 iter maybe O(n); okay. Could simplify source selection: top source from active gradient and random. For anneal diversity, random pairs enough. Use g for 50%:
- source list top `srcs` from valid by g; p=random choice from srcs.
- dest if random<0.5 random from valid; else bottom `dsts` from all by g.

But we can combine apply: In optimizer after pair line search returns best_x, call `new_b = move_b(a,b,p,q,x)` and update a. Function:
```
def apply_pair(a,b,p,q,x):
    L=2*n-1; diff=np.zeros(L); diff[p:p+n]-=a; diff[q:q+n]+=a
    new_b=b + 2*x*diff  # alloc
    new_b[2*p]+=x*x; ...
    a[p]-=x; if a[p]<0: a[p]=0
    a[q]+=x
    return new_b
```
This recomputes diff and adds, another O(n). Okay. Could have line search return final tmp for best_x to avoid recompute, but no. Cost double. Acceptable.

Pseudo code for greedy:
```
def greedy_pair_optimize(a, time_limit, rng, random_prob=0.05, max_candidates=8, fracs=None):
    n=len(a); a=normalize(a); b=fft_convolve(a); cur_obj=2n*b.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_b=b.copy(); best_obj=cur_obj
    t0=time.monotonic(); iter=0; stuck=0
    if fracs is None: fracs=(0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.55,0.8,0.97)
    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit:
       g=compute_direction(a,b, rc=8 if n>512 else 6)
       pairs=choose_pairs(g,a,rng,max_candidates,random_prob)
       best_pair=None; best_obj_pair=cur_obj
       for p,q in pairs:
          res=pair_line_search(a,b,p,q,cur_obj,fracs)
          if res is not None:
             obj,x=res
             if obj < best_obj_pair - 1e-12:
                best_obj_pair=obj; best_pair=(p,q,x)
       if best_pair is not None and best_obj_pair < cur_obj - 1e-12:
          p,q,x=best_pair
          new_b=apply_pair(a,b,p,q,x)
          b=new_b; cur_obj=best_obj_pair
          # due apply rounding, cur_obj may differ? keep direct? maybe recompute max of b
          cur_obj=2*n*b.max()
          if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
             best_obj=cur_obj; best_a=a.copy(); best_b=b.copy()
          stuck=0
       else:
          stuck += 1
          if stuck > 40:
             # random perturbation small or reset
             if stuck % 40 == 0 and best_a is not None:
                 # small random transfer
                 valid=np.flatnonzero(a > 1e-8)
                 if len(valid)>1:
                    p=int(valid[int(rng.integers(len(valid))))]
                    q=int(rng.integers(n))
                    if p != q:
                       x=min(0.02*a[p], 1e-3/n)
                       if x>0:
                          new_b=apply_pair(a,b,p,q,x)
                          b=new_b; cur_obj=2*n*b.max()
                stuck=0
             else:
                 a=best_a.copy(); b=best_obj? need best_b
       iter +=1
       if iter % 1000 == 0:
          a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s
          b=fft_convolve(a); cur_obj=2*n*b.max()
          if cur_obj < best_obj: update
    # Final recompute best? Return best_a,best_obj
```

`choose_pairs` implementation unique:
```
def choose_pairs(g,a,rng,max_pairs=8,random_prob=0.0):
    n=len(a); pairs=[]
    if n < 2: return pairs
    min_mass=max(1e-10,1e-4/n)
    valid=np.flatnonzero(a >= min_mass)
    if len(valid)==0:
       valid=np.nonzero(a>1e-12)[0]
    if len(valid)==0:
       valid=np.arange(n)
    if rng is not None and rng.random() < random_prob:
       idxs = rng.choice(len(valid), size=min(max_pairs,len(valid)), replace=False)
       srcs=valid[idxs]
       dsts=valid[rng.choice(len(valid), size=min(max_pairs,len(valid)), replace=False)]
    else:
       if len(valid) > max_pairs:
          srcs = valid[np.argpartition(-g[valid], max_pairs-1)[:max_pairs]]
       else: srcs=valid.copy()
       # dest all indices low g
       if n > max_pairs:
          dst_idx = np.argpartition(g, max_pairs-1)[:max_pairs]
       else: dst_idx = np.arange(n)
       dsts=dst_idx
    # limit each to maybe 4
    srcs = list(srcs[:4]); dsts=list(dsts[:4])
    for p in srcs:
       for q in dsts:
          p=int(p); q=int(q)
          if p != q and (p,a[p]>0? no) and (p,q not in pairs?)
             if not duplicate: pairs.append((p,q))
             if len(pairs)>=max_pairs: break
    if not pairs:
       # fallback random
    return pairs[:max_pairs]
```
Duplicate check simple loop.

Need no lambda; use for loops.

`compute_direction` details:
```
def compute_direction(a,b, rc=8):
    n=len(a)
    maxB = float(b.max())
    if maxB <= 0: return np.zeros(n)
    idx = np.argsort(b)[-rc:]
    # Optionally include all above threshold up to rc? Instead of top rc only.
    thresh = maxB - max(1e-12, 0.05*maxB)
    all_idx=np.where(b >= thresh)[0]
    if len(all_idx) > rc:
       # pick top rc from all_idx
       idx = all_idx[np.argpartition(b[all_idx], -rc)[-rc:]]
    else:
       idx = all_idx
    vals = b[idx]
    w = np.ones(len(idx))
    if len(idx) > 1:
       temp = max(1e-12, 0.1*(maxB - float(vals.min())))
       w = np.exp((vals - maxB)/temp)
       ss=w.sum()
       if ss>0: w /= ss
       else: w=np.ones(len(idx))/len(idx)
    g=np.zeros(n)
    for ii in range(len(idx)):
       k=int(idx[ii]); wt=float(w[ii])
       js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
       g[js:je+1] += wt * a[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
    return g
```
If top rc not include all near peaks? all_idx threshold 5% max, may be many for uniform? For uniform b triangular, max near center many? b values drop; threshold 0.95max includes O? For uniform max 1/n, values near center within 5% means |k-n+1| ≤? count roughly? b = (n-d)/n^2, threshold 0.9/n^2 => d≤0.1n, all_idx ~0.2n. Then pick top 8 central. Good. For plateau of peaks, picks top.

Maybe rc should scale: `rc = max(8, min(32, n//32))`? For n=2048 rc32 maybe gradient O(32*n) ~65k, ok. Use `rc = 12` fixed.

`choose_pairs` dest low g from all indices; if g zeros for all, argpartition arbitrary.

Now LP optimize details. It may modify a; we should copy input and keep best.
```
def lp_optimize(a, time_limit, max_calls=6, lp_max_n=1024):
    n=len(a)
    a=normalize(a.copy())
    b=fft_convolve(a); cur_obj=2*n*b.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=cur_obj
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or n > lp_max_n or time_limit <= 5: return best_a,best_obj
    t0=time.monotonic()
    calls=0
    t_grid=(0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.7,0.9)
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit and calls < max_calls:
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit - 2: break
       maxB=b.max()
       factors = (1.0, 1.05, 1.15, 1.3, 1.5)
       factor = factors[calls % len(factors)]
       M = maxB * factor + 1e-15
       g = lp_direction(a, M)
       if g is None:
          # maybe reduce n? break? If all fail, break
          if factor <=1.3: break? For now calls+=1; if calls>2 break
          continue
       # line search t
       best_t=0; best_obj_t=cur_obj; best_a_t=None
       for t in t_grid:
          h=(1-t)*a + t*g
          s=h.sum()
          if s>0: h = h/s
          hb=fft_convolve(h)
          obj=2*n*hb.max()
          if obj < best_obj_t - 1e-12:
             best_obj_t=obj; best_t=t; best_a_t=h.copy()
       if best_t > 0:
          a=best_a_t; b=fft_convolve(a); cur_obj=2*n*b.max()
          if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
             best_obj=cur_obj; best_a=a.copy()
       else:
          # no improvement with this g; try a slightly larger factor maybe next call
          pass
       calls += 1
       # If current best no improvement for 3 calls? break to save time
    if time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit-5:
       # final polish? Not here
    return best_a,best_obj
```

f_work = f.copy()

```
def mirror_optimize(a,time_limit, rng, max_steps? ):
   n=len(a); a=normalize; b=fft; cur=2n*max
   best...
   eta=0.5
   while time:
      g=compute_direction(a,b,rc=16)
      # center gradient to maintain sum? Multiplicative with centered g
      z = g - g.mean()
      best_h=a; best_eta=0; best_obj=cur
      for eta in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0):
          logh = -eta*z
          logh -= logh.max()
          h=a*np.exp(logh)
          s=h.sum(); if s>0: h/=s
          hb=fft(h); obj=2n*hb.max()
          if obj<best_obj: ...
      if best_eta>0 and best_obj<cur:
          a=best_h; b=fft; cur=best_obj; update best
          eta*=0.8
      else:
          eta/=2
      if no improve for 100: perturb or break
   return best
```

def improve_sequence(a,budget,rng,allow_lp):
   # initial quick greedy
   if budget > 3:
      t=min(budget*0.2, 60); a,obj=greedy_pair_optimize(a,t,rng,random_prob=0.05)

```
def mirror_optimize(a, time_limit, rng=None):
    n=len(a); a=normalize(a.copy()); b=fft; cur=2n*max
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=cur
    if time_limit <=1: return best
    t0=time.monotonic()
    eta=0.2
    no_imp=0
    while time...:
       g=compute_direction(a,b, rc=16)
       if not np.any(g): break
       z = g - np.mean(g)
       # Candidate etas decayed
       best_h=None; best_eta=0; best_obj=cur
       for eta_try in (eta*0.25, eta*0.5, eta, eta*2, eta*4):
           if eta_try <=0: continue
           log = -eta_try*z
           log -= log.max()
           h=a*np.exp(log)
           s=h.sum()
           if s <=0: continue
           h = h/s
           b2=fft_convolve(h)
           obj=2n*b2.max()
           if obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
               best_obj=obj; best_eta=eta_try; best_h=h.copy()
       if best_h is not None:
           a=best_h; b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max()
           if cur < best_obj: update
           no_imp=0
           eta = best_eta * 0.7
       else:
           no_imp += 1
           eta *= 0.5
           if no_imp > 50:
              # reset to best and perturb or break
              a=best_a.copy(); b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max(); no_imp=0; eta=0.3
              if no_imp? continue
           if eta < 1e-4:
              eta=0.1; no_imp=0; a=best_a.copy(); ...
       if every 1000 recompute
    if best? return
```

def direct_objective(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float); n=a.size
   if n==0: return inf
   s=a.sum()
   if s<1e-12: return inf
   b=fft_convolve(a/s)
   return float(2.0*n*b.max())
```

`fft_convolve` robust:
```
def fft_convolve(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    size=2*n-1
    nfft=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    out=np.fft.irfft(A*A, nfft)[:size]
    # remove negative roundoff
    np.maximum(out, 0.0, out=out)
    return out
```
For n=1, size1, nfft=1? `(1-1).bit_length()=0`, 1<<0=1. rfft length1 okay. Convolve [a0,a0]? Actually sequence length1, convolution length1 a0^2. A*A works. Good.

`normalize` should not mutate? Return new.
```
def normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).copy()
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    a /= s
    return a
```
If a empty? handle.

`generate_starts` details without lambdas:
```
def generate_starts(n, parent, rng, max_starts=8):
    starts=[]
    if parent is not None:
       starts.append(('parent', interpolate_sequence(parent,n)))
    starts.append(('uniform', np.ones(n)/n))
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    ts=(np.arange(n)+1.0)/float(n+1)
    p_vals=(0.25,0.5,1.5,4.0)
    for p in p_vals:
       base=np.sin(np.pi*ts)
       base=np.maximum(base,1e-12)**p
       base=normalize(base)
       starts.append(('sin'+str(p), base))
    for alpha in (1.0,3.0,8.0):
       base=np.exp(-alpha*x/float(n))
       base=normalize(base)
       starts.append(('geo'+str(alpha), base))
    g=np.exp(-0.5*((x-0.5*(n-1))/(0.25*max(1,n-1)))**2)
    starts.append(('gauss', normalize(g)))
    if rng is not None:
       # Instead of Dirichlet for large n maybe random positive
       base=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*0.5) if n<=2048 else random? Dirichlet with alpha .5 for 2048 can underflow? It handles.
       starts.append(('rand', base))
    if len(starts) > max_starts: starts=starts[:max_starts]
    return starts
```
For n=256 parent, geo alpha0? We might want less biased. Good.

Add to pool helper top-level? Could create `pool_append(pool, score, n, name, seq)` modifies list. Top-level.
```
def pool_append(pool, score, n, name, seq):
    if seq is None or not np.isfinite(score): return
    arr=np.asarray(seq,dtype=float)
    arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s<=1e-12: return
    pool.append((score, n, name, arr.copy()))
```
Sorting tuple with arrays at fourth: If first equal, compares n int, name string, then array? Python tuple comparison will compare arrays if first two equal, causing error. To avoid, ensure names unique enough or include unique counter. Use tuple `(score, counter, n, name, seq)` where counter int ensures no array compare. Or sort custom? Simpler: `pool.append([score, n, name, seq])` and sort key not lambda impossible. Could assign each entry an id: `(score, idx, n, name, seq)`. `idx` unique. Then tuple compare won't reach seq if idx unique. Use global idx counter in propose: `pool_id=0`, increment. `pool_append` can take idx? Top-level with idx. Implement safe_evaluate:
```
def safe_evaluate(a):
    arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size==0: return float('inf')
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)): return float('inf')
    s=arr.sum()
    if s < 0.01:
       # scale up to sum 1 to mimic? But evaluator would inf. For fallback maybe scale
    try:
       val=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
    except Exception:
       val=float('inf')
    if np.isfinite(val): return float(val)
    # fallback direct if evaluate not available or inf due sum? If sum<0.01, inf.
    if s < 0.01:
       return float('inf')
    b=fft_convolve(arr/s)
    return float(2.0*arr.size*b.max())
```

Potential issue with `np.argpartition` on very small arrays. In choose_pairs, if `max_pairs >= len(valid)`, use all. If n=1, return.
```
def choose_pairs(g,a,rng,max_pairs=8,random_prob=0.0):
    n=a.size
    pairs=[]
    if n<2 or max_pairs<=0: return pairs
    min_mass=max(1e-10,1e-4/float(n))
    valid=np.flatnonzero(a >= min_mass)
    if valid.size < 2:
       valid=np.flatnonzero(a > 1e-12)
    if valid.size == 0:
       valid=np.arange(n,dtype=int)
    max_pairs = min(max_pairs, n)
    if rng is not None and random_prob > 0.0 and rng.random() < random_prob:
       src_count = min(4, valid.size)
       dst_count = min(4, valid.size)
       srcs = valid[rng.choice(valid.size, size=src_count, replace=False)]
       dsts = valid[rng.choice(valid.size, size=dst_count, replace=False)]
    else:
       gs = g[valid]
       if valid.size > 4:
          srcs = valid[np.argpartition(-gs, 3)[:4]]
       else:
          srcs = valid.copy()
       if n > 4:
          dsts = np.argpartition(g, 3)[:4]
       else:
          dsts = np.arange(n)
    # limit
    srcs = np.asarray(srcs, dtype=int)[:4]
    dsts = np.asarray(dsts, dtype=int)[:4]
    for p in srcs:
       p=int(p)
       if a[p] <= 1e-12: continue
       for q in dsts:
          q=int(q)
          if p == q: continue
          # duplicate check
          dup=False
          for oldp,oldq in pairs:
             if oldp==p and oldq==q: dup=True; break
          if not dup:
             pairs.append((p,q))
             if len(pairs) >= max_pairs: return pairs
    if not pairs:
       if valid.size > 1:
          p=int(valid[0]); q=int(valid[1]);
          if p != q: pairs.append((p,q))
    return pairs
```
Need ensure `rng.choice` with `size=0` not; if valid.size=1 skipped earlier? If valid.size<2, fallback valid all; if n=1 return. If valid.size=1, src_count=1, dst_count=1, pair same maybe no; fallback.

`compute_direction`: Need if b has NaN? Should not. `np.argsort(b)[-rc:]` if rc > len b? rc <=? Use `rc = min(rc, b.size)`.
```
def compute_direction(a,b,rc=12):
   n=a.size; rc=min(rc, 2*n-1)
   maxB=float(b.max())
   if maxB <= 1e-300: return zeros
   if len(b) > rc:
      idx=np.argsort(b)[-rc:]
   else: idx=np.arange(b.size)
   # Optionally replace with near peaks? Top rc enough. But if many equal peaks, top arbitrary. okay.
   vals=b[idx]
   minv=float(vals.min())
   if len(idx)>1 and maxB - minv > 1e-300:
       temp=0.05*(maxB-minv)+1e-300
       w=np.exp((vals-maxB)/temp)
       wsum=w.sum()
       if wsum>0: w/=wsum
       else: w=np.ones(len(idx))/len(idx)
   else: w=np.ones(len(idx))/len(idx)
   g=np.zeros(n)
   for ii in range(len(idx)):
       k=int(idx[ii]); wt=float(w[ii])
       js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
       seg = a[k-je : k-js+1][::-1]
       g[js:je+1] += wt * seg
   return g
```
Using top rc only may ignore a peak with same max not selected; but top arbitrary among ties. Fine.

`pair_line_search`:
```
def pair_line_search(a,b,p,q,cur_obj,fracs=DEFAULT_FRACS,positive_only=False):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    mass=float(a[p])
    if p==q or mass <= 1e-15 or fracs is None or len(fracs)==0:
        return None
    diff=np.zeros(L)
    diff[p:p+n] -= a
    diff[q:q+n] += a
    lin=np.empty(L); tmp=np.empty(L)
    best_obj = cur_obj if not positive_only else float('inf')
    best_x=0.0
    for frac in fracs:
       x = float(frac)*mass
       if x <= 1e-18: continue
       np.multiply(diff, 2.0*x, out=lin)
       np.add(b, lin, out=tmp)
       tmp[2*p] += x*x
       tmp[2*q] += x*x
       if p != q:
           tmp[p+q] -= 2.0*x*x
       val = float(tmp.max())
       obj = 2.0*float(n)*val
       if obj < best_obj:
          best_obj=obj; best_x=x
    if best_x <= 0:
       return None
    return best_obj,best_x
```
For positive_only, if all candidates worse than cur, best_obj set to smallest candidate obj and x positive. Good. But if first candidate obj = inf? no. If mass positive.
Potential bug: `diff[p:p+n] -= a` where a length n. If p+n may exceed L? For p=n-1, p+n=2n-1 L, ok. If p=0,n ok. Good.
But `diff[q:q+n] += a` if q=n-1 length n. Good.

`apply_pair`:
```
def apply_pair(a,b,p,q,x):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    diff=np.zeros(L)
    diff[p:p+n] -= a
    diff[q:q+n] += a
    new_b=np.empty(L)
    np.multiply(diff, 2.0*x, out=new_b)
    np.add(b, new_b, out=new_b)
    new_b[2*p] += x*x
    new_b[2*q] += x*x
    if p != q:
       new_b[p+q] -= 2.0*x*x
    np.maximum(new_b, 0.0, out=new_b)
    a[p] = max(0.0, float(a[p]) - x)
    a[q] = float(a[q]) + x
    return new_b
```
But note `diff` uses old a, and then we modify a after computing new_b. Good.
If p==q no.

In greedy, after apply, `b=apply_pair` returns new based on old a; update a. Good.

Now `mirror_optimize` uses `compute_direction` and FFT. Need ensure if time_limit 0 returns.
```
def mirror_optimize(a, time_limit, rng=None):
    n=len(a); a=normalize(a.copy()); b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=cur
    if time_limit < 1.0: return a,cur
    t0=time.monotonic()
    eta=0.3
    noimp=0
    steps=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit-2: break
       g=compute_direction(a,b,rc=16)
       zg = g - np.mean(g)
       best_h=None; best_eta=0.0; best_obj=cur
       for mult in (0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0):
          et=float(eta*mult)
          if et <= 1e-8: continue
          logv = -et*zg
          m=logv.max()
          if not np.isfinite(m): break
          logv -= m
          h = a * np.exp(logv)
          s=h.sum()
          if s <= 1e-30: continue
          h = h / s
          b2=fft_convolve(h)
          obj=2.0*float(n)*float(b2.max())
          if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj - 1e-13:
             best_obj=obj; best_h=h.copy(); best_eta=et
       if best_h is not None:
          a=best_h; b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max()
          if cur < best_obj:
             best_obj=cur; best_a=a.copy()
          noimp=0; eta=best_eta*0.75
       else:
          noimp += 1
          eta *= 0.5
          if noimp > 40:
             a=best_a.copy(); b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max()
             noimp=0; eta=0.2
          if eta < 1e-5:
             eta=0.1; noimp=0; a=best_a.copy(); b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max()
       steps +=1
       if steps % 500 ==0:
          a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); a/=s
          b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max()
          if cur < best_obj: ...
    return best_a,best_obj
```
This can be slow if many FFTs; time cap. Good.

`greedy_pair_optimize`: Need use `apply_pair` and maybe recompute b every 500 moves to prevent drift. Use `best_a` and `best_b`.
```
def greedy_pair_optimize(a, time_limit, rng, random_prob=0.05, max_candidates=8):
    n=len(a); a=normalize(a.copy()); b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=cur
    t0=time.monotonic(); stuck=0; steps=0
    fracs=DEFAULT_FRACS
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit - 1: break
       g=compute_direction(a,b, rc=10 if n<=1024 else 14)
       pairs=choose_pairs(g,a,rng,max_candidates,random_prob)
       best_pair=None; best_pair_obj=cur
       for p,q in pairs:
          res=pair_line_search(a,b,p,q,cur)
          if res is not None:
             obj,x=res
             if obj < best_pair_obj - 1e-12:
                best_pair_obj=obj; best_pair=(p,q,x)
       if best_pair is not None and best_pair_obj < cur - 1e-12:
          p,q,x=best_pair
          b=apply_pair(a,b,p,q,x)
          cur=2.0*float(n)*float(b.max())
          if cur < best_obj - 1e-12:
             best_obj=cur; best_a=a.copy()
          stuck=0
       else:
          stuck +=1
          if stuck > 80:
             a=best_a.copy(); b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max(); stuck=0
          elif stuck > 10 and rng is not None:
             # random small move
             valid=np.flatnonzero(a > max(1e-8, 1e-4/n))
             if valid.size > 1:
                p=int(valid[int(rng.integers(valid.size))])
                q=int(rng.integers(n))
                if p != q and a[p] > 1e-10:
                   x=min(0.02*float(a[p]), 1e-3/float(n))
                   if x > 1e-15:
                      b=apply_pair(a,b,p,q,x)
                      cur=2n*b.max()
                      stuck=0
       steps +=1
       if steps % 500 ==0:
          a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s
          b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max()
          if cur < best_obj: best_obj=cur; best_a=a.copy()
    # final maybe if current better?
    if cur < best_obj: best_obj=cur; best_a=a.copy()
    return best_a,best_obj
```
Potential issue: when reset `a=best_a.copy()`, best_b not stored; recompute b. ok.
Random small x `1e-3/n` very tiny; may not escape. Use `x=min(0.02*a[p], 0.01/float(n))`? For n=256, 4e-5, mass small; could change objective. Use `0.05/float(n)` maybe 0.0002 (0.05 uniform mass). Too big for perturbation. But since it's random walk, okay. Use `x = min(0.02*a[p], 0.005/float(n))`. For n=256 2e-5. Fine. Or `x=0.01*float(a[p])`. Better relative to source mass; if source mass uniform 1/n, x=0.01/n. Use `x=min(0.01*float(a[p]), 0.005/float(n))`. Good.

Could use line search for random pair even if worse? In greedy no.

`pair_anneal`: Use `positive_only=True` and temperature. Need not use g every iter; recompute every 10.
```
def pair_anneal(a, time_limit, rng):
    n=len(a); a=normalize(a.copy()); b=fft; cur=2n*b.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=cur
    t0=time.monotonic(); steps=0; g=None
    fracs=ANNEAL_FRACS
    while time left:
       if steps % 10 ==0:
          g=compute_direction(a,b,rc=10)
       # choose pair
       if rng.random() < 0.5 and g is not None:
          valid=np.flatnonzero(a > max(1e-10,1e-4/n))
          if valid.size<2: valid=np.arange(n)
          gs=g[valid]
          if valid.size > 4:
             srcs=valid[np.argpartition(-gs,3)[:4]]
          else: srcs=valid
          p=int(srcs[int(rng.integers(len(srcs)))])
          if rng.random() < 0.5:
             q=int(valid[int(rng.integers(valid.size))])
          else:
             # low g all indices
             if n>4:
                lows=np.argpartition(g,3)[:4]
             else: lows=np.arange(n)
             q=int(lows[int(rng.integers(len(lows)))])
       else:
          p=int(rng.integers(n)); q=int(rng.integers(n))
       if p == q: continue
       res=pair_line_search(a,b,p,q,cur,fracs,positive_only=True)
       if res is None: continue
       cand_obj,x=res
       delta=cand_obj-cur
       frac = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-t0)/max(time_limit,1.0))
       T = 2.0e-4*(1.0-frac)**2 + 1.0e-6
       accept=False
       if delta < 0.0: accept=True
       else:
          if delta < 700.0*T:
             if rng.random() < math.exp(-delta/T): accept=True
       if accept:
          b=apply_pair(a,b,p,q,x)
          cur=2n*b.max()
          if cur < best_obj - 1e-12:
             best_obj=cur; best_a=a.copy()
       steps +=1
       if steps % 500 ==0: recompute normalize
    if cur < best_obj: ...
    return best_a,best_obj
```
For anneal, `pair_line_search positive_only` returns best fraction among fracs even if worse. This may choose huge fraction if all worse? It chooses min obj, likely small. Good.

Temperature scale: If objective ~1.5, delta for small move maybe 1e-6. T 2e-4 initial accepts many, 1e-6 end. Could be okay. We can maybe multiply by current obj? But objective absolute better. Use `T = 1e-4*(1-frac)^1.5 + 1e-7`.

`lp_optimize`: Need to track time and maybe LP directions. Use `lp_max_n` parameter. If `linprog` not defined, return current.
```
def lp_optimize(a, time_limit, max_calls=6, lp_max_n=1024):
    n=len(a); a=normalize(a.copy()); b=fft; cur=2n*b.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=cur
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or n > lp_max_n or time_limit < 10: return best_a,best_obj
    t0=time.monotonic()
    calls=0
    t_grid=(0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.7,0.9)
    factors=(1.0,1.05,1.15,1.3,1.6)
    no_improve=0
    while calls < max_calls and time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       # estimate time for LP? We just call. If time left <5 break
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit - 5: break
       M=float(b.max())*factors[calls % len(factors)] + 1e-15
       g=lp_direction(a,M)
       if g is None:
          no_improve += 1
          if no_improve > 2: break
          calls +=1
          continue
       best_h=None; best_t=0.0; best_obj_t=cur
       for t in t_grid:
           if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit - 3: break
           h=(1.0-t)*a + t*g
           h=np.maximum(h,0.0); s=h.sum()
           if s <=0: continue
           h=h/s
           b2=fft_convolve(h); obj=2n*b2.max()
           if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj_t - 1e-12:
              best_obj_t=obj; best_h=h.copy(); best_t=t
       if best_h is not None:
          a=best_h; b=fft_convolve(a); cur=2n*b.max()
          if cur < best_obj - 1e-12:
             best_obj=cur; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve=0
          else:
             no_improve += 1
       else:
          no_improve += 1
       calls +=1
       if no_improve >= 3:
          # break or continue with larger factor? factors cycle; break to save
          if calls >= 2: break
    return best_a,best_obj
```
But if no improvement with t=0.9 but t small? line search includes small. Good.
LP direction may be slow; if time_limit 60, max_calls maybe 3. Use in improve_sequence with cap.

`lp_direction` building C with f maybe not full after normalization. Use:
```
def lp_direction(f, M):
    n=f.size
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or n > 1024: return None
    f = np.asarray(f,dtype=float).copy()
    f = np.maximum(f,0.0)
    s=f.sum()
    if s <=0: return None
    # add tiny floor to avoid zero columns
    floor = 1e-14
    f = np.maximum(f, floor)
    f /= f.sum()
    L=2*n-1
    C=np.zeros((L,n), dtype=float)
    for k in range(L):
       js=...; je=...
       C[k, js:je+1] = f[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
    c=-np.ones(n)
    b_ub=np.full(L, M, dtype=float)
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=C, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=(0, None), method='highs', options={'output_flag':0, 'presolve':True, 'maxiter':100000})
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None or res.status != 0: return None
    g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
    g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
    gs=g.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(gs) or gs <= 1e-12: return None
    return g/gs
```
Use floor 1e-14; for n=1024 sum floor 1e-10 negligible. LP feasible.

Now `improve_sequence`: Need track best across methods. It can call methods with current best.
```
def improve_sequence(a, budget, rng, allow_lp=True):
    n=a.size; a=normalize(a.copy())
    start=time.monotonic()
    # compute initial best
    b=fft_convolve(a); best_obj=2n*b.max(); best_a=a.copy()
    # Define local helper to update? no nested. We'll write repeated after each method.
    # Phase 1: short greedy
    left = budget - (time.monotonic()-start)
    if left > 3:
       t=min(left*0.25, 60.0)
       a_temp,obj = greedy_pair_optimize(a,t,rng,random_prob=0.05)
       if obj < best_obj: best_obj=obj; best_a=a_temp.copy(); a=a_temp.copy()
    # LP
    left = budget - (time.monotonic()-start)
    if allow_lp and n <= 1024 and left > 15:
       t=min(left*0.30, 120.0)
       a_temp,obj=lp_optimize(a,t,max_calls=6)
       if obj < best_obj: ...
    # mirror
    left = budget - ...
    if left > 8:
       t=min(left*0.2, 45.0)
       a_temp,obj=mirror_optimize(a,t)
       if obj < best_obj: ...
    # greedy deep
    left=...
    if left > 4:
       t=min(left*0.35, 180.0)
       a_temp,obj=greedy_pair_optimize(a,t,rng,random_prob=0.02)
    # anneal
    left=...
    if left > 6:
       t=min(left*0.3, 120.0)
       a_temp,obj=pair_anneal(a,t,rng)
    # final greedy
    left=...
    if left > 2:
       a_temp,obj=greedy_pair_optimize(a,min(left-1,60.0),rng,random_prob=0.0)
    return best_a,best_obj
```
But if best_obj not updated after a method, we still use `a_temp` for further methods? `a_temp` may be worse but from current a; if method didn't improve over best, maybe still different. Use `a` as a_temp regardless? Could drift to worse. Better update `a` to a_temp even if not better? It may explore. But for final, return best. For continued methods, starting from a_temp (which is local optimum from that method) can be good even if obj slightly worse? Could hurt. Use if `obj < best_obj` or `obj <= cur_obj?` No current. Simpler: always set `a = a_temp.copy()` because methods return best within starting a, so obj <= starting obj (should). It may not be global best but better than input. def try_update_best(best_score, best_seq, seq, score):

Now implement `try_update_best`:
```
def try_update_best(best_score, best_seq, seq, score):
    if seq is None: return best_score,best_seq
    if not np.isfinite(score): return best_score,best_seq
    arr=np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0: return...
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)): return...
    arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return...
    if float(score) < float(best_score):
       return float(score), arr.copy()
    return best_score, best_seq
```

    n = a.size

Let's code `direct_score(a)` top-level for work:
```
def direct_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return inf
    b=fft_convolve(a/s)
    return float(2.0*float(n)*float(b.max()))
```

```python
"""
Hybrid optimizer for minimizing 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a))^2
with non-negative a.  The search combines:
  - active-constraint pair transfers with exact line search,
  - mirrosmoothing on the simplex,
  - trust-region directions solved by a linear program (when SciPy is available),
  - light annealing for escape from local minima.
The program normalizes to sum(a)=1 internally and returns a non-negative list.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

HAVE_SCIPY = False
try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    linprog = None

DEFAULT_FRACS = (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.55, 0.80, 0.97)
ANNEAL_FRACS = (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.40, 0.70)
LP_MAX_N = 1024


def fft_convolve(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    size = 2 * n - 1
    nfft = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    out = np.fft.irfft(A * A, nfft)[:size]
    np.maximum(out, 0.0, out=out)
    return out


def normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    a /= s
    return a


def direct_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    b = fft_convolve(a / s)
    return float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))


def safe_evaluate(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return float("inf")
    s = float(arr.sum())
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
    except Exception:
        val = float("inf")
    if np.isfinite(val):
        return float(val)
    if s >= 0.01:
        return float(2.0 * float(arr.size) * float(fft_convolve(arr / s).max()))
    return float("inf")


def try_update_best(best_score, best_seq, seq, score):
    if seq is None:
        return best_score, best_seq
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return best_score, best_seq
    arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return best_score, best_seq
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return best_score, best_seq
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return best_score, best_seq
    if float(score) < float(best_score):
        return float(score), arr.copy()
    return best_score, best_seq


def get_parent_sequence():
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if p.ndim != 1 or p.size == 0:
            return None
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            return None
        s = float(p.sum())
        if s <= 1e-12:
            return None
        return p / s
    except Exception:
        return None


def interpolate_sequence(parent, n):
    parent = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64)
    if parent.size <= 1:
        return np.full(n, float(parent[0]) if parent.size == 1 else 1.0)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, parent.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, parent)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return out / s


def generate_starts(n, parent, rng, max_starts=8):
    starts = []
    if parent is not None:
        starts.append(("parent", interpolate_sequence(parent, n)))
    starts.append(("uniform", np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)))

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    ts = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0) / float(n + 1)

    for p in (0.25, 0.5, 1.5, 4.0):
        base = np.sin(np.pi * ts)
        base = np.maximum(base, 1e-12) ** p
        starts.append(("sin_" + str(p), normalize(base)))

    for alpha in (1.0, 3.0, 8.0):
        base = np.exp(-alpha * x / float(n))
        starts.append(("geo_" + str(alpha), normalize(base)))

    if n > 1:
        mid = 0.5 * float(n - 1)
        sigma = 0.25 * float(n - 1)
        g = np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mid) / sigma) ** 2)
        starts.append(("gauss", normalize(g)))

    if rng is not None:
        base = rng.random(n)
        starts.append(("rand", normalize(base)))

    if len(starts) > max_starts:
        starts = starts[:max_starts]
    return starts


def compute_direction(a, b, rc=12):
    n = a.size
    rc = min(int(rc), b.size)
    if rc <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    maxB = float(b.max())
    if maxB <= 1e-300:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if b.size > rc:
        idx = np.argpartition(b, -rc)[-rc:]
    else:
        idx = np.arange(b.size)

    vals = b[idx]
    if len(idx) > 1:
        minv = float(vals.min())
        if maxB - minv > 1e-300:
            temp = 0.05 * (maxB - minv) + 1e-300
            w = np.exp((vals - maxB) / temp)
            ws = float(w.sum())
            if ws > 0.0 and np.isfinite(ws):
                w /= ws
            else:
                w = np.ones(len(idx), dtype=np.float64) / float(len(idx))
        else:
            w = np.ones(len(idx), dtype=np.float64) / float(len(idx))
    else:
        w = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for ii in range(len(idx)):
        k = int(idx[ii])
        wt = float(w[ii])
        js = max(0, k - n + 1)
        je = min(n - 1, k)
        if js <= je:
            g[js:je + 1] += wt * a[k - je:k - js + 1][::-1]
    return g


def choose_pairs(g, a, rng, max_pairs=8, random_prob=0.0):
    n = a.size
    pairs = []
    if n < 2 or max_pairs <= 0:
        return pairs

    min_mass = max(1e-10, 1e-4 / float(n))
    valid = np.flatnonzero(a >= min_mass)
    if valid.size < 2:
        valid = np.flatnonzero(a > 1e-12)
    if valid.size == 0:
        valid = np.arange(n, dtype=int)

    max_pairs = min(int(max_pairs), n)

    if rng is not None and random_prob > 0.0 and rng.random() < random_prob:
        cnt = min(4, int(valid.size))
        srcs = valid[rng.choice(valid.size, size=cnt, replace=False)]
        dsts = valid[rng.choice(valid.size, size=cnt, replace=False)]
    else:
        if valid.size > 4:
            gs = g[valid]
            srcs = valid[np.argpartition(-gs, 3)[:4]]
        else:
            srcs = valid.copy()

        if n > 4:
            dsts = np.argpartition(g, 3)[:4]
        else:
            dsts = np.arange(n, dtype=int)

    srcs = np.asarray(srcs, dtype=int)[:4]
    dsts = np.asarray(dsts, dtype=int)[:4]

    for p in srcs:
        p = int(p)
        if p < 0 or p >= n or a[p] <= 1e-12:
            continue
        for q in dsts:
            q = int(q)
            if q < 0 or q >= n or p == q:
                continue
            dup = False
            for op, oq in pairs:
                if op == p and oq == q:
                    dup = True
                    break
            if not dup:
                pairs.append((p, q))
                if len(pairs) >= max_pairs:
                    return pairs

    if not pairs and valid.size > 1:
        p = int(valid[0])
        q = int(valid[1])
        if p != q:
            pairs.append((p, q))
    return pairs


def pair_line_search(a, b, p, q, cur_obj, fracs=DEFAULT_FRACS, positive_only=False):
    p = int(p)
    q = int(q)
    n = a.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    mass = float(a[p])
    if p == q or mass <= 1e-15:
        return None
    if fracs is None or len(fracs) == 0:
        return None

    diff = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    diff[p:p + n] -= a
    diff[q:q + n] += a

    lin = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)
    tmp = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)

    best_obj = cur_obj if (not positive_only) else float("inf")
    best_x = 0.0

    for frac in fracs:
        x = float(frac) * mass
        if x <= 1e-18:
            continue

        np.multiply(diff, 2.0 * x, out=lin)
        np.add(b, lin, out=tmp)

        tmp[2 * p] += x * x
        tmp[2 * q] += x * x
        if p != q:
            tmp[p + q] -= 2.0 * x * x

        val = float(tmp.max())
        obj = 2.0 * float(n) * val
        if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj:
            best_obj = obj
            best_x = x

    if best_x <= 0.0:
        return None
    return best_obj, best_x


def apply_pair(a, b, p, q, x):
    p = int(p)
    q = int(q)
    n = a.size
    L = 2 * n - 1

    diff = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    diff[p:p + n] -= a
    diff[q:q + n] += a

    new_b = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)
    np.multiply(diff, 2.0 * float(x), out=new_b)
    np.add(b, new_b, out=new_b)

    new_b[2 * p] += float(x) * float(x)
    new_b[2 * q] += float(x) * float(x)
    if p != q:
        new_b[p + q] -= 2.0 * float(x) * float(x)
    np.maximum(new_b, 0.0, out=new_b)

    a[p] = max(0.0, float(a[p]) - float(x))
    a[q] = float(a[q]) + float(x)
    return new_b


def greedy_pair_optimize(a, time_limit, rng, random_prob=0.05, max_candidates=8):
    n = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).size
    a = normalize(a.copy())
    b = fft_convolve(a)
    cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
    best_obj = cur_obj
    best_a = a.copy()

    if time_limit <= 0.0:
        return best_a, best_obj

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    stuck = 0
    steps = 0
    rc = 12 if n > 1024 else 10

    while True:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
        if elapsed >= time_limit or elapsed > time_limit - 1.0:
            break

        g = compute_direction(a, b, rc=rc)
        pairs = choose_pairs(g, a, rng, max_candidates=msg if False else max_candidates)
        # fix accidental macro above: choose_pairs(..., max_candidates=max_candidates)
        pairs = choose_pairs(g, a, rng, max_candidates=max_candidates, random_prob=random_prob)

        best_pair = None
        best_pair_obj = cur_obj

        for p, q in pairs:
            res = pair_line_search(a, b, p, q, cur_obj, fracs=DEFAULT_FRACS, positive_only=False)
            if res is not None:
                obj, x = res
                if obj < best_pair_obj - 1e-12:
                    best_pair_obj = obj
                    best_pair = (p, q, x)

        if best_pair is not None and best_pair_obj < cur_obj - 1e-12:
            p, q, x = best_pair
            b = apply_pair(a, b, p, q, x)
            cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
            if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
                best_obj = cur_obj
                best_a = a.copy()
            stuck = 0
        else:
            stuck += 1
            if stuck > 100:
                a = best_a.copy()
                b = fft_convolve(a)
                cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
                stuck = 0
            elif stuck > 10 and rng is not None:
                min_mass = max(1e-10, 1e-4 / float(n))
                valid = np.flatnonzero(a > min_mass)
                if valid.size > 1:
                    p = int(valid[int(rng.integers(valid.size))])
                    q = int(rng.integers(n))
                    if p != q and a[p] > 1e-12:
                        x = min(0.01 * float(a[p]), 0.005 / float(n))
                        if x > 1e-15:
                            b = apply_pair(a, b, p, q, x)
                            cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
                            stuck = 0

        steps += 1
        if steps % 500 == 0:
            a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
            s = float(a.sum())
            if s > 0.0:
                a /= s
            b = fft_convolve(a)
            cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
            if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
                best_obj = cur_obj
                best_a = a.copy()

    if cur_obj < best_obj:
        best_obj = cur_obj
        best_a = a.copy()
    return best_a, best_obj


def mirror_optimize(a, time_limit, rng=None):
    n = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).size
    a = normalize(a.copy())
    b = fft_convolve(a)
    cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
    best_obj = cur_obj
    best_a = a.copy()

    if time_limit <= 1.0:
        return best_a, best_obj

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    eta = 0.3
    no_imp = 0
    steps = 0

    while True:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
        if elapsed >= time_limit or elapsed > time_limit - 2.0:
            break

        g = compute_direction(a, b, rc=16)
        zg = g - float(np.mean(g))
        best_h = None
        best_eta = 0.0
        best_candidate_obj = cur_obj

        for mult in (0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
            et = float(eta) * float(mult)
            if et <= 1e-9:
                continue
            logv = -et * zg
            m = float(logv.max())
            if not np.isfinite(m):
                continue
            logv -= m
            h = a * np.exp(logv)
            s = float(h.sum())
            if s <= 1e-30:
                continue
            h = h / s
            b2 = fft_convolve(h)
            obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b2.max()))
            if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_candidate_obj - 1e-13:
                best_candidate_obj = obj
                best_h = h.copy()
                best_eta = et

        if best_h is not None:
            a = best_h
            b = fft_convolve(a)
            cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
            if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
                best_obj = cur_obj
                best_a = a.copy()
            no_imp = 0
            eta = max(1e-5, best_eta * 0.75)
        else:
            no_imp += 1
            eta = max(1e-5, eta * 0.5)
            if no_imp > 40:
                a = best_a.copy()
                b = fft_convolve(a)
                cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
                no_imp = 0
                eta = 0.2
            if eta < 1e-5:
                eta = 0.1
                no_imp = 0
                a = best_a.copy()
                b = fft_convolve(a)
                cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))

        steps += 1
        if steps % 500 == 0:
            a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
            s = float(a.sum())
            if s > 0.0:
                a /= s
            b = fft_convolve(a)
            cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
            if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
                best_obj = cur_obj
                best_a = a.copy()

    return best_a, best_obj


def pair_anneal(a, time_limit, rng):
    n = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).size
    a = normalize(a.copy())
    b = fft_convolve(a)
    cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
    best_obj = cur_obj
    best_a = a.copy()

    if time_limit <= 0.0 or rng is None:
        return best_a, best_obj

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    steps = 0
    g = None

    while True:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
        if elapsed >= time_limit or elapsed > time_limit - 1.0:
            break

        if steps % 10 == 0:
            g = compute_direction(a, b, rc=10)

        if g is not None and rng.random() < 0.5:
            min_mass = max(1e-10, 1e-4 / float(n))
            valid = np.flatnonzero(a > min_mass)
            if valid.size < 2:
                valid = np.arange(n, dtype=int)
            if valid.size > 4:
                gs = g[valid]
                srcs = valid[np.argpartition(-gs, 3)[:4]]
            else:
                srcs = valid
            p = int(srcs[int(rng.integers(srcs.size))])
            if rng.random() < 0.5:
                q = int(valid[int(rng.integers(valid.size))])
            else:
                if n > 4:
                    lows = np.argpartition(g, 3)[:4]
                else:
                    lows = np.arange(n, dtype=int)
                q = int(lows[int(rng.integers(lows.size))])
        else:
            p = int(rng.integers(n))
            q = int(rng.integers(n))

        if p == q:
            continue

        res = pair_line_search(a, b, p, q, cur_obj, fracs=ANNEAL_FRACS, positive_only=True)
        if res is None:
            continue
        cand_obj, x = res

        frac_time = min(1.0, elapsed / max(time_limit, 1.0))
        T = 2.0e-4 * ((1.0 - frac_time) ** 2) + 1.0e-6
        delta = float(cand_obj) - float(cur_obj)

        accept = False
        if delta < 0.0:
            accept = True
        else:
            if T > 0.0:
                ratio = delta / T
                if ratio < 700.0:
                    if rng.random() < math.exp(-ratio):
                        accept = True

        if accept:
            b = apply_pair(a, b, p, q, x)
            cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
            if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
                best_obj = cur_obj
                best_a = a.copy()

        steps += 1
        if steps % 500 == 0:
            a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
            s = float(a.sum())
            if s > 0.0:
                a /= s
            b = fft_convolve(a)
            cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
            if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
                best_obj = cur_obj
                best_a = a.copy()

    return best_a, best_obj


def lp_direction(f, M):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = f.size
    if n <= 0 or n > LP_MAX_N:
        return None
    f = np.maximum(f, 1e-14)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return None
    f /= s

    L = 2 * n - 1
    C = np.zeros((L, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(L):
        js = max(0, k - n + 1)
        je = min(n - 1, k)
        if js <= je:
            C[k, js:je + 1] = f[k - je:k - js + 1][::-1]

    c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.full(L, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
    try:
        res = linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=C,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=(0.0, None),
            method="highs",
            options={"output_flag": 0, "presolve": True, "maxiter": 120000},
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is None or res.status != 0:
        return None

    g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    gs = float(g.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(gs) or gs <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return g / gs


def lp_optimize(a, time_limit, max_calls=6, lp_max_n=LP_MAX_N):
    n = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).size
    a = normalize(a.copy())
    b = fft_convolve(a)
    cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
    best_obj = cur_obj
    best_a = a.copy()

    if not HAVE_SCIPY or n > lp_max_n or time_limit < 10.0:
        return best_a, best_obj

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    calls = 0
    no_improve = 0
    t_grid = (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.70, 0.90)
    factors = (1.02, 1.08, 1.20, 1.40, 1.70)

    while calls < max_calls:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
        if elapsed >= time_limit or elapsed > time_limit - 6.0:
            break

        M = float(b.max()) * float(factors[calls % len(factors)]) + 1e-12
        g = lp_direction(a, M)
        if g is None:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 2:
                break
            calls += 1
            continue

        best_h = None
        best_t = 0.0
        best_candidate_obj = cur_obj

        for t in t_grid:
            elapsed2 = time.monotonic() - t0
            if elapsed2 >= time_limit or elapsed2 > time_limit - 3.0:
                break
            h = (1.0 - t) * a + t * g
            h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
            s = float(h.sum())
            if s <= 0.0:
                continue
            h = h / s
            b2 = fft_convolve(h)
            obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b2.max()))
            if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_candidate_obj - 1e-12:
                best_candidate_obj = obj
                best_h = h.copy()
                best_t = float(t)

        if best_h is not None:
            a = best_h
            b = fft_convolve(a)
            cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b.max()))
            if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
                best_obj = cur_obj
                best_a = a.copy()
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                no_improve += 1
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        calls += 1
        if no_improve >= 3 and calls >= 2:
            break

    return best_a, best_obj


def improve_sequence(a, budget, rng, allow_lp=True):
    n = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).size
    a0 = normalize(a.copy())
    b0 = fft_convolve(a0)
    cur_obj = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b0.max()))
    best_obj = cur_obj
    best_a = a0.copy()
    cur_a = a0.copy()

    t_start = time.monotonic()

    left = float(budget) - (time.monotonic() - t_start)
    if left > 2.0:
        t1 = min(left * 0.20, 60.0)
        if t1 > 0.5:
            a_new, obj_new = greedy_pair_optimize(cur_a, t1, rng, random_prob=0.05)
            if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < cur_obj - 1e-12:
                cur_a = a_new.copy()
                cur_obj = float(obj_new)
                if cur_obj < best_obj:
                    best_obj = cur_obj
                    best_a = cur_a.copy()

    left = float(budget) - (time.monotonic() - t_start)
    if allow_lp and HAVE_SCIPY and n <= LP_MAX_N and left > 15.0:
        t2 = min(left * 0.30, 130.0)
        if t2 > 5.0:
            a_new, obj_new = lp_optimize(cur_a, t2, max_calls=6)
            if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < cur_obj - 1e-12:
                cur_a = a_new.copy()
                cur_obj = float(obj_new)
                if cur_obj < best_obj:
                    best_obj = cur_obj
                    best_a = cur_a.copy()

    left = float(budget) - (time.monotonic() - t_start)
    if left > 8.0:
        t3 = min(left * 0.20, 60.0)
        if t3 > 1.0:
            a_new, obj_new = mirror_optimize(cur_a, t3, rng)
            if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < cur_obj - 1e-12:
                cur_a = a_new.copy()
                cur_obj = float(obj_new)
                if cur_obj < best_obj:
                    best_obj = cur_obj
                    best_a = cur_a.copy()

    left = float(budget) - (time.monotonic() - t_start)
    if left > 4.0:
        t4 = min(left * 0.40, 360.0)
        if t4 > 1.0:
            a_new, obj_new = greedy_pair_optimize(cur_a, t4, rng, random_prob=0.02)
            if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < cur_obj - 1e-12:
                cur_a = a_new.copy()
                cur_obj = float(obj_new)
                if cur_obj < best_obj:
                    best_obj = cur_obj
                    best_a = cur_a.copy()

    left = float(budget) - (time.monotonic() - t_start)
    if left > 8.0:
        t5 = min(left * 0.30, 240.0)
        if t5 > 1.0:
            a_new, obj_new = pair_anneal(cur_a, t5, rng)
            if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < cur_obj - 1e-12:
                cur_a = a_new.copy()
                cur_obj = float(obj_new)
                if cur_obj < best_obj:
                    best_obj = cur_obj
                    best_a = cur_a.copy()

    left = float(budget) - (time.monotonic() - t_start)
    if left > 2.0:
        t6 = max(0.0, left - 1.0)
        if t6 > 0.5:
            a_new, obj_new = greedy_pair_optimize(cur_a, t6, rng, random_prob=0.0)
            if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < cur_obj - 1e-12:
                cur_a = a_new.copy()
                cur_obj = float(obj_new)
                if cur_obj < best_obj:
                    best_obj = cur_obj
                    best_a = cur_a.copy()

    return best_a, best_obj


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t_start + max(0.0, budget_s - 30.0)

    parent = get_parent_sequence()

    best_score = float("inf")
    best_seq = None
    pool = []
    pool_id = 0

    if budget_s >= 1500.0:
        base_lengths = (256, 512, 1024, 2048)
    elif budget_s >= 700.0:
        base_lengths = (256, 512, 1024)
    elif budget_s >= 300.0:
        base_lengths = (256, 512)
    else:
        base_lengths = (256,)

    scan_lengths = list(base_lengths)
    if parent is not None:
        pn = int(parent.size)
        if 64 <= pn <= 4096 and pn not in scan_lengths:
            scan_lengths.append(pn)
    scan_lengths.sort()

    phase1_budget = min(300.0, max(30.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.12))
    phase1_deadline = min(time.monotonic() + phase1_budget, deadline)

    for n in scan_lengths:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now > phase1_deadline:
            break
        starts_per_len = 5 if n >= 1024 else 7
        starts = generate_starts(n, parent, rng, max_starts=starts_per_len)
        total_entries = max(1, len(scan_lengths) * starts_per_len)
        time_per = max(0.5, phase1_budget / float(total_entries))

        for name, a0 in starts:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now > phase1_deadline:
                break
            a0 = normalize(a0)
            b0 = fft_convolve(a0)
            score0 = float(2.0 * float(n) * float(b0.max()))
            if np.isfinite(score0):
                best_score, best_seq = try_update_best(best_score, best_seq, a0, score0)
                pool.append((score0, pool_id, n, name, a0.copy()))
                pool_id += 1

            remaining_phase = phase1_deadline - time.monotonic()
            tfast = min(time_per, remaining_phase - 0.1)
            if tfast > 1.0:
                a_opt, obj_opt = greedy_pair_optimize(a0, tfast, rng, random_prob=0.05)
                if np.isfinite(obj_opt):
                    best_score, best_seq = try_update_best(best_score, best_seq, a_opt, obj_opt)
                    pool.append((obj_opt, pool_id, n, name, a_opt.copy()))
                    pool_id += 1

    if not pool:
        fb = np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64) / 128.0
        fs = direct_score(fb)
        best_score, best_seq = try_update_best(best_score, best_seq, fb, fs)
        pool.append((fs, pool_id, 128, "fallback", fb.copy()))
        pool_id += 1

    pool.sort()
    top_pool = pool[:12]

    selected = []
    seen_keys = set()
    max_selected = 3
    for entry in top_pool:
        key = (int(entry[2]), str(entry[3]))
        if key not in seen_keys:
            seen_keys.add(key)
            selected.append(entry)
            if len(selected) >= max_selected:
                break

    reserve_final = 300.0 if budget_s > 1200.0 else 60.0
    remaining_for_deep = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - reserve_final)

    if selected and remaining_for_deep > 10.0:
        deep_time_each = remaining_for_deep / float(len(selected))
        for entry in selected:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now > deadline - reserve_final:
                break
            a_entry = np.asarray(entry[4], dtype=np.float64).copy()
            allow = (a_entry.size <= LP_MAX_N)
            a_opt, obj_opt = improve_sequence(a_entry, deep_time_each, rng, allow_lp=allow)
            if np.isfinite(obj_opt):
                best_score, best_seq = try_update_best(best_score, best_seq, a_opt, obj_opt)
                pool.append((obj_opt, pool_id, int(a_opt.size), str(entry[3]) + "_deep", a_opt.copy()))
                pool_id += 1

    pool.sort()

    # Verify a small set of top candidates with the actual evaluator.
    for entry in pool[:12]:
        cand = np.asarray(entry[4], dtype=np.float64)
        sc = safe_evaluate(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            best_score, best_seq = try_update_best(best_score, best_seq, cand, sc)

    # Final polish on the current best, then verify again at the very end.
    final_reserve = 20.0
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic() - final_reserve
    work_seq = None

    if best_seq is not None and remaining > 5.0:
        work_seq = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        work_score = direct_score(work_seq)
        nwork = int(work_seq.size)

        if nwork <= LP_MAX_N and HAVE_SCIPY and remaining > 20.0:
            t_lp = min(90.0, remaining * 0.20)
            if t_lp > 5.0:
                a_new, obj_new = lp_optimize(work_seq, t_lp, max_calls=3)
                if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < work_score - 1e-12:
                    work_seq = a_new.copy()
                    work_score = float(obj_new)

        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic() - final_reserve
        if remaining > 5.0:
            t_g1 = min(remaining * 0.45, 360.0)
            if t_g1 > 1.0:
                a_new, obj_new = greedy_pair_optimize(work_seq, t_g1, rng, random_prob=0.0)
                if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < work_score - 1e-12:
                    work_seq = a_new.copy()
                    work_score = float(obj_new)

        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic() - final_reserve
        if remaining > 10.0:
            t_anneal = min(remaining * 0.30, 150.0)
            if t_anneal > 1.0:
                a_new, obj_new = pair_anneal(work_seq, t_anneal, rng)
                if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < work_score - 1e-12:
                    work_seq = a_new.copy()
                    work_score = float(obj_new)

        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic() - final_reserve
        if remaining > 2.0:
            t_g2 = max(0.0, remaining - 1.0)
            if t_g2 > 0.5:
                a_new, obj_new = greedy_pair_optimize(work_seq, t_g2, rng, random_prob=0.0)
                if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new < work_score - 1e-12:
                    work_seq = a_new.copy()
                    work_score = float(obj_new)

    # Final safe verification over a few candidates.
    candidates = []
    if best_seq is not None:
        candidates.append(np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64))
    if work_seq is not None:
        candidates.append(np.asarray(work_seq, dtype=np.float64))
    pool.sort()
    for entry in pool[:8]:
        candidates.append(np.asarray(entry[4], dtype=np.float64))

    final_best_score = float("inf")
    final_best_seq = None
    for cand in candidates:
        if cand is None:
            continue
        cand = np.asarray(cand, dtype=np.float64)
        if cand.size == 0:
            continue
        sc = safe_evaluate(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < final_best_score:
            final_best_score = sc
            final_best_seq = cand.copy()

    if final_best_seq is None:
        fb = np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64) / 128.0
        final_best_score = safe_evaluate(fb)
        final_best_seq = fb

    final_arr = np.asarray(final_best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    final_arr = np.clip(final_arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(final_arr.sum())
    if s > 1e-12:
        final_arr = final_arr / s

    return [float(x) for x in final_arr]
```
